Instead, my mind is just completely consumed by my work-related anxieties and small daily tasks, like whether I locked my front door.\n\nDoctor: Before you started this new role 15 months ago, did you have a history of being a frequent worrier, or is this level of persistent anxiety entirely new to you?"

Instead, my mind is just completely consumed by my work-related anxieties and small daily tasks, like whether I locked my front door.\n\nDoctor: Before you started this new role 15 months ago, did you have a history of being a frequent worrier, or is this level of persistent anxiety entirely new to you?\n\nPatient: This level of constant, overwhelming anxiety is entirely new to me and only started after I took on the new role fifteen months ago. While I've always been a meticulous planner who likes to stay highly organized, I have never experienced this kind of uncontrollable worry and physical tension before.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from work, such as on weekends or during vacations, does this intense anxiety and physical tension completely go away, or does it persist even then?"
  }

No, it definitely doesn't go away, even on weekends or if I try to take a trip. I still find myself obsessively checking my work emails and mentally rehearsing worst-case scenarios about my deliverables. Instead, my mind is just completely consumed by my work-related anxieties and small daily tasks, like whether I locked my front door.\n\nDoctor: Before you started this new role 15 months ago, did you have a history of being a frequent worrier, or is this level of persistent anxiety entirely new to you?\n\nPatient: This level of constant, overwhelming anxiety is entirely new to me and only started after I took on the new role fifteen months ago. While I've always been a meticulous planner who likes to stay highly organized, I have never experienced this kind of uncontrollable worry and physical tension before.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from work, such as on weekends or during vacations, does this intense anxiety and physical tension completely go away, or does it persist even then?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely doesn't go away, even on weekends or if I try to take a trip. I still find myself obsessively checking my work emails and mentally rehearsing worst-case scenarios about my deliverables. I’ve even started turning down plans with friends because I feel too wired and anxious to actually relax and enjoy myself.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling anxious, do you ever experience physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those specific symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach. It's more of a constant, heavy physical tension and just feeling incredibly 'wired' all the time."

I have stopped doing things I normally enjoy, like running with my running club and going out with friends, but it's because I feel too wired and overwhelmed by work to actually show up and enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about other areas of your life, such as your family, health, or minor daily tasks, or is your anxiety mainly focused on your work and job performance?\n\nPatient: While work is definitely the biggest trigger, my worry absolutely spills over into minor daily tasks. My brain is constantly spinning, and I'll obsess over things like whether I remembered to lock my apartment door, to the point where I feel like I can never genuinely relax.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to control or stop these anxious thoughts and worries once they start?\n\nPatient: Yes, it feels almost impossible to stop them once they get going. Even if I try to distract myself by watching a show or reading, those \"what if\" thoughts sneak back in within minutes. They just snowball from one worst-case scenario to another until I feel completely hijacked by them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing this constant worry and tension on most days over the last 15 months, or does it tend to come and go?\n\nPatient: It definitely doesn't come and go. It has been my near-daily, non-stop normal ever since I started this new role about fifteen months ago, and I honestly can't remember the last time I felt genuinely relaxed.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling exhausted, have you been experiencing physical symptoms like muscle tension, headaches, or trouble falling and staying asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes, my sleep is awful—I lie awake until 2 or 3 a.m. replaying work emails in my head, and even when I do sleep, I wake up constantly and feel like I'm dragging my body through molasses the next day. I also carry a lot of constant physical tension in my body, though I don't really get headaches.\n\nDoctor: Besides work and minor daily chores, do you find yourself worrying about other major areas of your life, such as your health, finances, or the safety and well-being of your family?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really worry about things like my health, finances, or my family's safety. Instead, my mind is just completely consumed by my work-related anxieties and small daily tasks, like whether I locked my front door.\n\nDoctor: Before you started this new role 15 months ago, did you have a history of being a frequent worrier, or is this level of persistent anxiety entirely new to you?\n\nPatient: This level of constant, overwhelming anxiety is entirely new to me and only started after I took on the new role fifteen months ago. While I've always been a meticulous planner who likes to stay highly organized, I have never experienced this kind of uncontrollable worry and physical tension before.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from work, such as on weekends or during vacations, does this intense anxiety and physical tension completely go away, or does it persist even then?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely doesn't go away, even on weekends or if I try to take a trip. I still find myself obsessively checking my work emails and mentally rehearsing worst-case scenarios about my deliverables. I’ve even started turning down plans with friends because I feel too wired and anxious to actually relax and enjoy myself.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling anxious, do you ever experience physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those specific symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach. It's more of a constant, heavy physical tension and just feeling incredibly 'wired' all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming more irritable, impatient, or easily frustrated with others since these feelings of anxiety and tension started?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed feeling more irritable or impatient with others. My struggle is much more internal, and I tend to focus all of that energy inward on my own exhaustion and constant worry."

While I've always been a meticulous planner who likes to stay highly organized, I have never experienced this kind of uncontrollable worry and physical tension before.\n\nDoctor: When you are away from work, such as on weekends or during vacations, does this intense anxiety and physical tension completely go away, or does it persist even then?\n\nPatient: No, it definitely doesn't go away, even on weekends or if I try to take a trip. I still find myself obsessively checking my work emails and mentally rehearsing worst-case scenarios about my deliverables. I’ve even started turning down plans with friends because I feel too wired and anxious to actually relax and enjoy myself.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling anxious, do you ever experience physical symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those specific symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, shaking, or an upset stomach. It's more of a constant, heavy physical tension and just feeling incredibly 'wired' all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming more irritable, impatient, or easily frustrated with others since these feelings of anxiety and tension started?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed feeling more irritable or impatient with others. The only thing I really consume is a single cup of coffee in the morning to try and help me wake up."
